Appendix F
Proof of Theorem
4.6

Our proof of Theorem
4.6
in the present Appendix
F
shows that if
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
satisfies Properties
7
,
7
,
7
, and
7
, then
{\bm{\delta}}
is a Pareto cut. Our proof makes use of the notions of ‘Pareto cuts’ and ‘domination’ that are given in Definition
4.2
(Section
4.1
) as well as makes use of Theorem
4.5
(Section
4.3
).

We begin our proof of Theorem
4.6
with the following straightforward lemma.

Lemma F.1

Let
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
. We have that
{\bm{\delta}}
is a Pareto cut if and only if there does not exist
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
that satisfies Properties
7
,
7
,
7
, and
7
and dominates
{\bm{\delta}}
.

Proof F.2

Proof of Lemma
F.1
.
Let
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
. If
{\bm{\delta}}
is a Pareto cut, then it follows from Definition
4.2
that there does not exist
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
that dominates
{\bm{\delta}}
, which proves the first direction of Lemma
F.1
. To prove the other direction, suppose that
{\bm{\delta}}
is not a Pareto cut. In that case, it follows from Definition
4.2
that there exists
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
that dominates
{\bm{\delta}}
, in the sense that

J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})\text{ for all }{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
,
where that inequality is strict at some
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. It follows from Theorem
4.5
that either
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Properties
7
,
7
,
7
, and
7
or there exists
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
that satisfies Properties
7
,
7
,
7
, and
7
and dominates
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
. Therefore, we conclude in the latter case that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime\prime}
satisfies Properties
7
,
7
,
7
, and
7
and dominates
{\bm{\delta}}
, which completes the proof of the other direction of Lemma
F.1
.
\halmos

Equipped with the above lemma, we now present the organization of the proof of Theorem
4.6
. Indeed, consider any
{\bm{\delta}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
that satisfy Properties
7
,
7
,
7
, and
7
.
Our proof of Theorem
4.6
is split into five cases, which are shown in the following table.

Case

If…

then
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
, as shown in …

1

\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}>\delta_{L_{k}+1}

Proposition
F.3
in Appendix
F.1

2

\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{2}^{\prime}<\delta_{2}

Proposition
F.7
in Appendix
F.2.1

3

\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{2}^{\prime}\geq\delta_{2}

Proposition
F.9
in Appendix
F.2.2

4

\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})}^{\prime}<\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}

Proposition
F.13
in Appendix
F.3

5

\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})}^{\prime}=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}

Proposition
F.17
in Appendix
F.4

We observe that the five regimes in the second column are mutually exclusive and collectively exhaustive.
16
16

16

Note that the case where
\delta_{T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})}^{\prime}>\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}
does not need to be considered because
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
implies that
\delta_{T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})}^{\prime}\leq\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}
.

Consequently, we conclude that Propositions
F.3
-
F.17
in combination with Lemma
F.1
completes the proof of Theorem
4.6
. The remainder of Appendix
F
consists of proving Propositions
F.3
-
F.17
.

F.1
Case 1

Our first case consists of proving the following proposition.

Proposition F.3

If
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}>\delta_{L_{k}+1}
, then
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
.

Proof F.4

Proof of Proposition
F.3
.
Assume that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}>\delta_{L_{k}+1}
.
Let
{\bm{x}}
be the vector that is defined for each
\ell\in[N+1]
as

\displaystyle x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\ell=L_{k}+1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

We observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)0=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)0=\delta_{L_{k}+1}

which implies that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})>J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

Since
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}
, we have shown that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
.

\halmos

F.2
Cases 2 and 3

Our proofs in Appendices
F.2.1
and
F.2.2
will make use of the following lemma.

Lemma F.5

If
\hat{{\bm{\delta}}}\in\Delta_{k}
satisfies Property
7
, then for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
we have

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},\hat{{\bm{\delta}}})

\displaystyle=\hat{\delta}_{L_{k}+1}+\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\hat{\delta}_{2}\right)x_{i_{k,1}}+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\hat{\delta}_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\hat{\delta}_{\ell+1}-\hat{\delta}_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

Proof F.6

Proof of Lemma
F.5
.
Note that the inequality
1<L_{k}+1
follows from Assumption
2.3
(see Section
2.3
). Let
\hat{{\bm{\delta}}}\in\Delta_{k}
satisfy Property
7
. We observe for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that

\displaystyle\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\hat{\delta}_{1}\right\}-\left(\hat{\delta}_{2}-\hat{\delta}_{1}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\left(\max\left\{\hat{\delta}_{1},r_{i_{k,1}}\right\}-\hat{\delta}_{2}\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\hat{\delta}_{2}\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

where the first equality follows from algebra and the second equality follows from the fact that
\hat{{\bm{\delta}}}
satisfies Property
7
. The above reasoning together with the definition of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},\hat{{\bm{\delta}}})
completes the proof of Lemma
F.5
.

\halmos

Equipped with Lemma
F.5
, we proceed to prove Case 2 and Case 3.

F.2.1
Case 2.

Our second case consists of proving the following proposition.

Proposition F.7

If
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{2}^{\prime}<\delta_{2}
, then
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
.

Proof F.8

Proof of Proposition
F.7
.
Assume that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{2}^{\prime}<\delta_{2}
. It follows from the fact that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{2}^{\prime}<\delta_{2}
that
L_{k}+1>2
.
Let
{\bm{x}}
be the vector that is defined for each
\ell\in[N+1]
as

\displaystyle x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\ell\in\{1,L_{k}+1\},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

We observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta^{\prime}_{2}\right)1+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle>\delta_{L_{k}+1}+r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{2}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{2}\right)1+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle=J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

The first equality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
and from Lemma
F.5
as well as the construction of
{\bm{x}}
. The second equality follows from algebra. The inequality follows from the fact that
\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta^{\prime}_{2}<\delta_{2}
. The third equality follows from algebra. The fourth equality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
and from Lemma
F.5
as well as the construction of
{\bm{x}}
. Since
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}
, we have shown that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
.
\halmos

F.2.2
Case 3.

Our third case consists of proving the following proposition.

Proposition F.9

If
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{2}^{\prime}\geq\delta_{2}
, then
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
.

Our proof of Proposition
F.9
will utilize the following lemma.

Lemma F.10

If
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{2}^{\prime}\geq\delta_{2}
, then either
(\delta_{2},\ldots,\delta_{L_{k}+1})=(\delta_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime})
or there exists
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
such that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}-\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}<\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}
.

Proof F.11

Proof of Lemma
F.10
.
Assume that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{2}^{\prime}\geq\delta_{2}
. We recall from Assumption
2.3
(see Section
2.3
) that
L_{k}\geq 1
, and the lemma clearly holds if
L_{k}=1
. We thus assume throughout the rest of the proof of Lemma
F.10
that
L_{k}\geq 2
. Suppose that
(\delta_{2},\ldots,\delta_{L_{k}+1})\neq(\delta_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime})
and

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}-\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}\geq\delta_{\ell^{\prime}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{\prime}}\;\forall\ell^{\prime}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}.

(34)

In that case, we observe that

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}-\delta^{\prime}_{2}=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=2}^{L_{k}}\left(\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}-\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}\right)\geq\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=2}^{L_{k}}\left(\delta_{\ell^{\prime}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{\prime}}\right)=\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\delta_{2}.

(35)

We recall that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta^{\prime}_{2}\geq\delta_{2}
, and so it follows from (
35
) that

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}-\delta^{\prime}_{2}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\delta_{2}.

(36)

The only way that (
34
) and (
36
) can be true simultaneously is for the equality
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}+1}-\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}=\delta_{\ell^{\prime}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{\prime}}
to hold for all
\ell^{\prime}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
. Since
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
, it must be the case that

(\delta_{2},\ldots,\delta_{L_{k}+1})=(\delta_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime})
, which is a contradiction. This concludes the proof of Lemma
F.10
.

\halmos

Equipped with Lemma
F.10
, we now present our proof of Proposition
F.9
.

Proof F.12

Proof of Proposition
F.9
.
Assume that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{2}^{\prime}\geq\delta_{2}
. If
(\delta_{2},\ldots,\delta_{L_{k}+1})=(\delta_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime})
, then it follows from Lemma
F.5
and from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
and
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfy Property
7
that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})=J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
, which implies that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
. Therefore, we assume henceforth that
(\delta_{2},\ldots,\delta_{L_{k}+1})\neq(\delta_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime})
, and so it follows from Lemma
F.10
that there exists
\ell^{*}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
that satisfies
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}}<\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}
. Let
{\bm{x}}
be the vector that is defined for each
\ell\in[N+1]
as

\displaystyle x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\ell\in\{\ell^{*},L_{k}+1\},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

We observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle={\delta}_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-{\delta}_{2}^{\prime}\right)0+\sum_{\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}\setminus\{\ell^{*}\}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-{\delta}_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left({\delta}_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-{\delta}_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right)\right)1

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+0-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle>\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)

The first equality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
and from Lemma
F.5
as well as the construction of
{\bm{x}}
. The second equality and the first inequality follow from algebra. The third equality holds because
\delta_{L_{k}+1}=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}
. The second inequality follows from the definition of
\ell^{*}
.
Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle={\delta}_{L_{k}+1}+\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-{\delta}_{2}\right)0+\sum_{\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}\setminus\{\ell^{*}\}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-{\delta}_{\ell}\right\}-\left({\delta}_{\ell+1}-{\delta}_{\ell}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)\right)1

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+0-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)

The first equality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
and from Lemma
F.5
as well as the construction of
{\bm{x}}
. The second equality follows from algebra. The third equality holds because
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
and because
0\leq\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}}<\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}
and because
\ell^{*}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
, which together imply that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}
. Combining the above reasoning, we have shown that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})>\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)=J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

Since
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}
, we have shown that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
.

\halmos

F.3
Case 4

Our fourth case consists of proving the following proposition.

Proposition F.13

If
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})}^{\prime}<\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}
, then
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
.

Proof F.14

Proof of Proposition
F.13
.
Assume that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})}^{\prime}<\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}
. For notational convenience, let
\ell^{*}\triangleq T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
. We will make use of the following two intermediary claims.

Claim 5

\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}}<\delta_{\ell^{*}}\leq r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}
.

Proof F.15

Proof of Claim
5
.
We observe that

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}=\delta_{\ell^{*}}\leq r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}

The first inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
. The second inequality follows from assumption. The equality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
and the fact that
\ell^{*}=T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
. The third inequality follows from the definition of
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
and the fact that
\ell^{*}=T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
. That completes the proof of Claim
5
.

\halmos

Claim 6

\ell^{*}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
.

Proof F.16

Proof of Claim
6
.
We observe that

\displaystyle r_{i_{k,L_{k}+1}}=0\leq\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}}<\delta_{\ell^{*}}

where the equality follows from the fact that
i_{k,L_{k}+1}=N+1
, the first inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
, and the second inequality follows from Claim
5
. This shows that
\ell^{*}<L_{k}+1
. Moreover, it also follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
that
\ell^{*}\geq 2
. That completes the proof of Claim
6
.

\halmos

Equipped with the above intermediary claims, we now proceed to prove Proposition
F.13
. Indeed, let
{\bm{x}}
be the vector that is defined for each
\ell\in[N+1]
as

\displaystyle x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\ell\in\{\ell^{*},L_{k}+1\},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Note that it follows from Claim
6
that
\ell^{*}<L_{k}+1
. We observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}\}\setminus\{\ell^{*}\}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right)\right)1

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\left(r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right)-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle>\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}+r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}

The first equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{x}}
, and the second equality follows from algebra. The third equality follows from Claim
5
. The fourth equality follows from algebra. The fifth equality follows from the fact that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}}<r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}
(Claim
5
) and the fact that
\ell^{*}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
(Claim
6
), which together with the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
implies that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}+1}=\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}}
. The inequality follows from the assumption that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}<\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}
, and the sixth equality follows from algebra. Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}\}\setminus\{\ell^{*}\}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)\right)1

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\left(r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\left(r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}\right)-\left(\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}

The first equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{x}}
, and the second equality follows from algebra. The third equality follows from Claim
5
. The fourth equality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
and the fact that
\ell^{*}=T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
, which imply that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}=\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}=\cdots=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
. The fifth equality follows from algebra. Combining the above reasoning, we have shown that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})>r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}=J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}).

Since
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}
, we have shown that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
.

\halmos

F.4
Case 5

Our fifth case consists of proving the following proposition.

Proposition F.17

If
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})}^{\prime}=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}
, then
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
.

Proof F.18

Proof of Proposition
F.17
.
Assume that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
and
\delta_{T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})}^{\prime}=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}
. For notational convenience, let
\ell^{*}\triangleq T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
.
We will utilize the following three intermediary claims.

Claim 7

\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}=\cdots=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}=\cdots=\delta_{\ell^{*}}\leq r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}
.

Proof F.19

Proof of Claim
7
.
The fact that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}}=\cdots=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}
follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
and from the assumption that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}}=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}
. The fact that
\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
is an assumption. The fact that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}=\cdots=\delta_{\ell^{*}}
follows from the fact that
\ell^{*}=T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
and from the assumption that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
. The fact that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}\leq r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}
follows from the fact that
\ell^{*}=T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
and from the definition of
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
. This completes our proof of Claim
7
.

\halmos

Claim 8

\ell^{*}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
.

Proof F.20

Proof of Claim
8
. It follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
and from the fact that
\ell^{*}=T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
that
\ell^{*}\geq 2
. Now suppose for the sake of developing a contradiction that
\ell^{*}=L_{k}+1
. If that were true, then it would follow from the fact that
\ell^{*}=T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
and from the definition of
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}\leq r_{i_{k,L_{k}+1}}=0
. However, we assumed that
\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
, which would imply that
\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}<0
, which contradicts the assumption that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
. Therefore, we have proven by contradiction that
\ell^{*}\leq L_{k}
, which completes our proof of Claim
8
.

\halmos

Claim 9

If
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}
is a vector that satisfies
x_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}=1
and
x_{i_{k,\ell}}=0

\forall\ell\in\{\ell^{*}+1,\ldots,L_{k}\}
, then

\displaystyle\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=\ell^{*}}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell=\ell^{*}}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}

Proof F.21

Proof of Claim
9
.
Let
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}
be a vector that satisfies
x_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}=1
and
x_{i_{k,\ell}}=0
for all
\ell\in\{\ell^{*}+1,\ldots,L_{k}\}
. Then

\displaystyle\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=\ell^{*}}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=\ell^{*}+1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)\right)1

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\left(r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\left(r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}\right)-\left(\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}

The first equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{x}}
and from Claim
8
(which implies that
\ell^{*}\leq L_{k}
). The second equality follows from algebra. The third equality follows from the fact that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}\leq r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}
(Claim
7
), and the fourth equality follows from the fact that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}=\cdots=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
(Claim
7
). The fifth equality follows from algebra. We similarly observe that

\displaystyle\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell=\ell^{*}}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell=\ell^{*}+1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right)\right)1

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\left(r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right)-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\left(r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}\right)-\left(\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}

The first equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{x}}
and from Claim
8
(which implies that
\ell^{*}\leq L_{k}
). The second equality follows from algebra. The third equality follows from the fact that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}<r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}
(Claim
7
), and the fourth equality follows from the fact that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}=\cdots=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}
(Claim
7
). The fifth equality follows from algebra.
That concludes the proof of Claim
9
.

\halmos

In view of the above intermediary claims, the remainder of the proof of Proposition
F.17
is split into two parts.

•

Part 1
: Suppose that
\delta_{2}^{\prime}<\delta_{2}
. In that case, let
{\bm{x}}
be the vector that is defined for each
\ell\in[N+1]
as

\displaystyle x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\ell\in\{1,\ell^{*},L_{k}+1\},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

We note in the above definition that the set
\{1,\ell^{*},L_{k}+1\}
is well defined because
\ell^{*}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
(Claim
8
). We observe that
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}
and that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{*}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}^{\prime}-\delta_{1}^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}+\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}^{\prime}\right)-\left(\delta_{2}^{\prime}-\delta_{1}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}+r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle>r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}+r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{2}

The first equality follows from the definition of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})
and from Claim
9
. The second equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{x}}
. The third equality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
(which implies that
\delta_{1}^{\prime}\leq r_{i_{k,1}}
). The fourth equality follows from algebra. The inequality follows from the supposition that
\delta_{2}^{\prime}<\delta_{2}
. Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{*}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}+\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right)-\left(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}+r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{2}

The first equality follows from the definition of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
and from Claim
9
. The second equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{x}}
. The third equality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
(which implies that
\delta_{1}\leq r_{i_{k,1}}
). The fourth equality follows from algebra.

Combining the above reasoning, we have shown that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})>r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}+r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{2}\geq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}).

Since
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}
, we have shown that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
in Part 1.

•

Part 2
: Suppose that
\delta_{2}^{\prime}\geq\delta_{2}
. In that case, define the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\triangleq\left\{\ell\in\{2,\ldots,\ell^{*}-1\}:\delta_{\ell}<\delta_{\ell+1}\right\}

We begin by proving that
\mathcal{L}
is nonempty. Indeed, it follows from Claim
8
that
\ell^{*}\geq 2
. Moreover, it follows from Claim
7
that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}<\delta_{\ell^{*}}
. Combining the inequalities
\ell^{*}\geq 2
and
\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}<\delta_{\ell^{*}}
together with the supposition that
\delta^{\prime}_{2}\geq\delta_{2}
, we conclude that
\ell^{*}\geq 3
. We have thus shown that the set
\{2,\ldots,\ell^{*}-1\}
is nonempty. Furthermore, it follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
and from Claim
7
that
\delta_{2}^{\prime}\leq\cdots\leq\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}<\delta_{\ell^{*}}
. It thus follows from the supposition that
\delta_{2}\leq\delta_{2}^{\prime}
that there must exist at least one
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,\ell^{*}-1\}
that satisfies
\delta_{\ell}<\delta_{\ell+1}
. We have thus shown that
\mathcal{L}
is nonempty.

Let
{\bm{x}}
be the vector that is defined for each
\ell\in[N+1]
as

\displaystyle x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\ell\in\mathcal{L}\cup\{\ell^{*},L_{k}+1\},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

We observe that
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}
. We also observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{*}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq-\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\geq-\sum_{\ell=2}^{\ell^{*}-1}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}-\delta_{2}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle>-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}}-\delta_{2}\right)

(37)

The first equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{x}}
. The first inequality follows from algebra. The second inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
and the fact that
\mathcal{L}\subseteq\{2,\ldots,\ell^{*}-1\}
. The second equality follows from algebra. The third inequality follows from Claim
7
(which implies that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}}<\delta_{\ell^{*}}
) and from the supposition that
\delta^{\prime}_{2}\geq\delta_{2}
. We further observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{*}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\ell=2}^{\ell^{*}-1}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}}-\delta_{2}\right)

(38)

The first equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{x}}
. The second equality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
and from the fact that
\delta_{\ell}<\delta_{\ell+1}
for all
\ell\in\mathcal{L}\subseteq\{2,\ldots,\ell^{*}-1\}
, which implies that
\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}}
for all
\ell\in\mathcal{L}
. The third equality follows from the fact that
\delta_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell+1}
for all
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,\ell^{*}-1\}\setminus\mathcal{L}
. The fourth equality follows from algebra.

Combining the above reasoning, we have shown that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})-J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{*}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\ell^{*}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle>-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}}-\delta_{2}\right)-\left(-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}}-\delta_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=0

where the first equality follows from Claim
9
and the definitions of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})
and
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
, the inequality follows from lines (
37
) and (
38
), and the second equality follows from algebra.

Since
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}
, we have shown that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
does not dominate
{\bm{\delta}}
in Part 2.

Because Parts 1 and 2 are exhaustive, our proof of Proposition
F.17
is complete.

\halmos

Appendix G
Proof of Proposition
4.7

Let
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
. We recall from Section
4.1
that
\mathcal{R}_{k}
is the set of unique revenues among the products that are not less preferred by ranking
k
to the no-purchase option, and that
\bar{r}_{k}\triangleq\max\{r\in\mathcal{R}_{k}\}
denotes the highest revenue in
\mathcal{R}_{k}
. Following the notation from Subroutine 1, let
\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}\triangleq\max\{r\in\mathcal{R}_{k}:r<\bar{r}_{k}\}
denote the second-highest revenue in
\mathcal{R}_{k}
. It is shown in Section
4.1
that
|\mathcal{R}_{k}|\geq 2
, which implies that
\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}
is always finite and that there always exists
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
that satisfies
r_{i_{k,\ell}}=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}
.

If
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})>1
, then it follows from Subroutine 1 that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}={\bm{\delta}}
, which implies that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
and satisfies Property
7
. Therefore, assume from this point onward that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})=1
. It follows from that assumption and from the definition of
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
that
\delta_{1}\leq r_{i_{k,1}}
and that
r_{i_{k,\ell}}<\delta_{\ell}\leq\bar{r}_{k}
for all
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
. For those inequalities to be true, it must be the case that
r_{i_{k,1}}=\bar{r}_{k}
and it must be the case that
r_{i_{k,2}},\ldots,r_{i_{k,L_{k}+1}}\leq\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}
.

Now let
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
be defined as in Subroutine 1; that is, let
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
be the vector defined by

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}\triangleq\min\{\delta_{\ell},\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}\}\quad\forall\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}

It follows readily from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
. Furthermore, it follows from the fact that
r_{i_{k,2}},\ldots,r_{i_{k,L_{k}+1}}\leq\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}
that there exists
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
such that
r_{i_{k,\ell}}=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}
. It thus follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}\leq r_{i_{k,\ell}}
for that
\ell
, which implies that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})>1
. We have thus shown that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

The remainder of the proof of Proposition
4.7
is organized as follows. In Appendix
G.1
, we show that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. In Appendix
G.2
, we show that there always exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
. The combination of
Appendices
G.1
and
G.2
thus implies that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
dominates
{\bm{\delta}}
.

G.1
Proof that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}

In the present Appendix
G.1
, we show that

J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. To this end, consider any
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. We first observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L_{k}}\left(0-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}+\left(\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right)-\left(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}+\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

where the first equality is the definition of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
, and the second equality follows from algebra. The third equality follows from the fact that
r_{i_{k,\ell}}<\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
and from the fact that
\delta_{1}\leq r_{i_{k,1}}
. The fourth equality follows from algebra. Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{L_{k}+1}\right\}+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell}\right\}\right\}-\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell+1}\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell}\right\}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{1}\right\}\right\}-\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{2}\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{1}\right\}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell}\right\}\right\}-\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell+1}\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell}\right\}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{1}\right\}\right\}-\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{2}\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{1}\right\}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L_{k}}\left(0-\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell+1}\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell}\right\}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{1}\right\}\right\}-\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{2}\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{1}\right\}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\sum_{\ell=2}^{L_{k}}\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell+1}\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell}\right\}\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{1}\right\}\right)-\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{2}\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{1}\right\}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell+1}\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell}\right\}\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}+\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{1}\right\}\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

The first equality is the definition of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})
. The second equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
. The third equality holds because it must be the case that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}>\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}
, since if that inequality did not hold, then it would follow from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
that there would exist
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
such that
r_{i_{k,\ell}}=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}
and
\delta_{\ell}\leq r_{i_{k,\ell}}
. The fourth equality holds because
r_{i_{k,\ell}}<\delta_{\ell}
and
r_{i_{k,\ell}}\leq\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}
for all
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
. The fifth equality holds because
r_{i_{k,1}}=\bar{r}_{k}>\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}
and because
r_{i_{k,1}}\geq\delta_{1}
. The sixth equality follows from algebra.

Therefore,

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})-J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle=\left(\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}\right)-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell+1}\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime},\delta_{1}\right\}\right)-\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell+1},0\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell},0\right\}\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}-\min\left\{\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}-\delta_{1},0\right\}x_{i_{k,1}}

(39)

We have two cases to consider.

•

First, suppose that
\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}<\delta_{1}\leq\cdots\leq\delta_{L_{k}+1}
. In that case, we have

(
39
)

\displaystyle=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\left(\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell+1}\right)-\left(\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}-\left(\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}-\delta_{1}\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}-\left(\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}-\delta_{1}\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)1-\left(\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}-\delta_{1}\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{1}-\left(\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}-\delta_{1}\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{1}-\left(\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}-\delta_{1}\right)1

\displaystyle=0

where the first equality follows from the supposition that
\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}<\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
, the second equality follows from algebra, the first inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
(which implies that
x_{i_{k,\ell}}\leq 1
for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
) and the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
(which implies that
\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\geq 0
for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}\}
). The third equality follows from algebra. The second inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
(which implies that
x_{i_{k,1}}\leq 1
) and the supposition that
\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}<\delta_{1}
. The fourth equality follows from algebra.

•

Second, suppose that
\delta_{1}\leq\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}
. In that case, it follows from the fact that
\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
that there exists
L\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}\}
such that
\delta_{L}\leq\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}<\delta_{L+1}
. In that case, we observe that

(
39
)

\displaystyle=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell+1},0\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell},0\right\}\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L+1},0\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L},0\right\}\right)x_{i_{k,L}}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\ell=L+1}^{L_{k}}\left(\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell+1},0\right\}-\min\left\{\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell},0\right\}\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}-\min\left\{\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}-\delta_{1},0\right\}x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(0-0\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\left(\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L+1}\right)-0\right)x_{i_{k,L}}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\ell=L+1}^{L_{k}}\left(\left(\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell+1}\right)-\left(\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}-\left(\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}-\delta_{1}\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\left(\delta_{L+1}-\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}\right)x_{i_{k,L}}+\sum_{\ell=L+1}^{L_{k}}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}+\left(\delta_{1}-\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\left(\delta_{L+1}-\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}\right)1+\sum_{\ell=L+1}^{L_{k}}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)1+\left(\delta_{1}-\bar{r}^{\prime}_{k}\right)0

\displaystyle=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}-\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\left(\delta_{L+1}-\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}\right)+\left(\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\delta_{L+1}\right)

\displaystyle=0

where the first equality follows from algebra. The second equality follows from fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
and the fact that
\delta_{L}\leq\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}<\delta_{L+1}
, which together imply that
\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{L}\leq\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}<\delta_{L+1},\ldots,\delta_{L_{k}+1}
. The inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
(which implies that
x_{i_{k,L}},\ldots,x_{i_{k,L_{k}+1}}\leq 1
and
x_{i_{k,1}}\geq 0
), the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
(which implies that
\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\geq 0
for all
\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}\}
), and the fact that
\delta_{1}\leq\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}<\delta_{L+1}
. The fourth and fifth equalities follow from algebra.

Since the above two cases are exhaustive, we conclude that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})-J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})\leq 0
.

G.2
Proof that there exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})

In the present Appendix
G.2
, we show that there exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
. To this end, let
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
be the vector that is defined for each
\ell\in[N+1]
as

\displaystyle\bar{x}_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\ell=L_{k}+1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

It follows from the discussion in the beginning of Appendix
G
that there exists
\ell^{*}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
that satisfies
r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}
and that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}>r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}
. We observe for any such
\ell^{*}
that

\displaystyle J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{L_{k}+1},\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}\right\}

\displaystyle<\delta_{L_{k}+1}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle=J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})

The first equality follows from the construction of
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
. The second equality follows from algebra and from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
. The inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
(which implies that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}\geq\delta_{\ell^{*}}
) and the fact that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})=1
and
\ell^{*}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
(which implies that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}>r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}=\bar{r}_{k}^{\prime}
). The third equality follows from algebra. The fourth equality follows from the construction of
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
. This completes our proof that there always exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
.

Appendix H
Proof of Proposition

Let
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
satisfy Property

, and let

be the output of Subroutine 2. If
\delta_{1}\leq r_{i_{k,1}}
, then it follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}={\bm{\delta}}
, which implies that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
and satisfies Properties
7
and
7
. Therefore, assume from this point onward that
\delta_{1}>r_{i_{k,1}}
, in which case we observe that the output

of Subroutine 2 satisfies

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}+1\},\\
r_{i_{k,1}},&\text{if }\ell=1.\end{cases}

We readily observe that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
. Moreover, it follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property

that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property

. The vector

also obviously satisfies Property

.

The remainder of the proof of Proposition

is organized as follows. In Appendix

, we show that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. In Appendix

, we show that there always exists

. The combination of Appendices

and

thus implies that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}

.

H.1
Proof that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}

In the present Appendix
H.1
, we show that

J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. To this end, consider any
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. We observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})-J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle=\left(\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-r_{i_{k,1}}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}-r_{i_{k,1}}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,1}}-\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\left(0-\left(\delta_{2}-r_{i_{k,1}}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,1}}-\left(0-\left(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\left(r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right)x_{i_{k,1}}

\displaystyle\leq 0

where the first equality follows from the definitions of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})
and
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
, the second equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
, the third equality follows from algebra and from the supposition that
\delta_{1}>r_{i_{k,1}}
, the fourth equality follows from algebra, and the inequality follows from the supposition that
\delta_{1}>r_{i_{k,1}}
and the fact that
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. We thus conclude that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})-J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})\leq 0
.

H.2
Proof that there exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})

In the present Appendix
H.2
, we show that there exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
. To this end, let
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
be the vector that is defined for each
\ell\in[N+1]
as

\displaystyle\bar{x}_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\ell\in\{1,L_{k}+1\},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

It follows from Assumption
2.3
from Section
2.3
that
L_{k}\geq 1
, which implies that
1\neq L_{k}+1
. We observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=2}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}^{\prime}-\delta_{1}^{\prime}\right)\right)1

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle<\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}\right)\right)1

\displaystyle=J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})

The first equality follows from the construction of
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
. The second equality follows from algebra and from the fact that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
. The third equality follows from the fact that
0=r_{i_{k,1}}-r_{i_{k,1}}=r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta^{\prime}_{1}>r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}
, which implies that
\max\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta^{\prime}_{1}\}=\max\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\}
. The inequality follows from the fact that
\delta_{1}^{\prime}<\delta_{1}
. The fourth equality follows from algebra, and the fifth equality follows from the construction of
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
. This completes our proof that there always exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
.

Appendix I
Proof of Proposition
4.9

Let
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
satisfy Properties
7
and
7
, and let
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
be the output of Subroutine 3. We will make use of the following lemma.

Lemma I.1

If
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
, then
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}={\bm{\delta}}
.

Proof I.2

Proof of Lemma
I.1
.
Suppose that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
.
It follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
that
\delta^{\prime}_{1}=\delta_{1}
. In what follows, we prove that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
by backward induction on
\ell
. In the base case where
\ell=L_{k}+1
, it follows immediately from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
that
\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
. Now consider any arbitrary
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
, and assume by induction that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}=\delta_{\ell^{\prime}}
for all
\ell^{\prime}\in\{\ell+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
. In the case of
\ell^{\prime}=\ell
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}},\\
\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}\right\}&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}},\\
\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta_{\ell+1}\right\}&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}},\\
\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta_{\ell}\right\}&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\ell}

where the first equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
, the second equality follows from the induction hypothesis, the third equality follows from the fact that
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
and from the supposition that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
, and the fourth equality follows from algebra. Since
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
was chosen arbitrarily, our proof of Lemma
I.1
is complete.

\halmos

It follows from Lemma
I.1
that if
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
, then
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}={\bm{\delta}}
, which implies that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
and satisfies Properties
7
,
7
, and
7
. Therefore, assume from this point onward that
{\bm{\delta}}
does not satisfy Property
7
. Our proof will also make use of the following intermediary lemma.

Lemma I.3

\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
.

Proof I.4

Proof of Lemma
I.3
.
It follows immediately from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
that
\delta^{\prime}_{1}=\delta_{1}
. In what follows, we prove that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
by backwards induction on
\ell
. In the base case where
\ell=L_{k}+1
, it follows immediately from construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
. Now consider any arbitrary
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
, and assume by induction that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}\geq\delta_{\ell^{\prime}}
for all
\ell^{\prime}\in\{\ell+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
. In the case of
\ell^{\prime}=\ell
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}},\\
\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}\right\}&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\geq\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}},\\
\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta_{\ell+1}\right\}&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\geq\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}},\\
\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta_{\ell}\right\}&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\ell}

The first equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
. The first inequality follows from the induction hypothesis. The second inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
(which implies that
\delta_{\ell+1}\geq\delta_{\ell}
). The second equality follows from algebra.
Since
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
was chosen arbitrarily, our proof of Lemma
I.3
is complete.

I.1
Proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}

In the present Appendix
I.1
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
. Indeed, it follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
and from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
that
\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}\leq\bar{r}_{k}
, and it follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
and from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
that
\delta^{\prime}_{1}=\delta_{1}\geq 0
. We observe that

\delta^{\prime}_{1}=\delta_{1}\leq\delta_{2}\leq\delta_{2}^{\prime}

where the equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
, the first inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
, and the second inequality follows from Lemma
I.3
. Finally, for all
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}},\\
\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\right\},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell+1},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}},\\
\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\right\},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}},\\
\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\right\},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}},\\
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}

where the first equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
, the first inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
(which implies that
\delta_{\ell}\leq\delta_{\ell+1}
), the second inequality follows from Lemma
I.3
, the third inequality follows from algebra, and the second equality follows from algebra. We have thus shown that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
.

I.2
Proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7

In the present Appendix
I.2
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. Indeed, it follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})>1
, which implies that there exists
\ell^{*}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
such that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}\leq r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}
. If

, then it follows from the construction of

that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}}=\delta_{\ell^{*}}\leq r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}=0
, which implies that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})=L_{k}+1>1
. If

, then it follows from the fact that
\ell^{*}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
that

\displaystyle\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell^{*}},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell^{*}}=r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}},\\
\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}},\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}+1}\right\},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell^{*}}<r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}}

The equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
and from the definition of
\ell^{*}
(which implies that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}\leq r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}}})
. The inequality follows from algebra. This shows that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\geq\ell^{*}\geq 2
, which implies that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. This completes our proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

Proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}

7

In the present Appendix
I.3
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}

. Indeed, we observe that

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle=\delta_{1}\leq r_{i_{k,1}}

where the equality follows from the construction of

. We have thus shown that
