I.4
Proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7

In the present Appendix
I.4
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. Indeed, for each
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
, we observe that

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}}\\
\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\right\},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}}\\
r_{i_{k,\ell}},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\text{ and }r_{i_{k,\ell}}<\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\\
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1},&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\text{ and }r_{i_{k,\ell}}\geq\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\end{cases}

(40)

where the first equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
and the second equality follows from algebra. We now consider each of the above three cases. If
\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, then it follows from line (
40
) that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, which complies with Property
7
. If
\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}
and
r_{i_{k,\ell}}<\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}
, then it follows from line (
40
) that
\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, which complies with Property
7
. If
\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}
and
r_{i_{k,\ell}}\geq\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}
, then
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}=\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}
, which complies with Property
7
.

Since this reasoning holds for all
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
, we have shown that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

I.5
Proof that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}

In the present Appendix
I.5
, we show that

J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. We will make use of the following intermediary lemma.

Lemma I.5

For all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\leq\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)

Proof I.6

Proof of Lemma
I.5
.
Consider any
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}\}
. If
\ell=1
, then we observe that

\displaystyle\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}^{\prime}-\delta_{1}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}^{\prime}-\delta_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,1}}-\delta_{1}\right\}-\left(\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}\right)

The equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
(which implies that
\delta^{\prime}_{1}=\delta_{1}
). The inequality follows Lemma
I.3
. If
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
, then we observe that

\displaystyle\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right),&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}},\\
\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\right\}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\right\}\right),&\text{if }\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}\end{cases}

where the inequality follows from Lemma
I.3
and the equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
. If
\delta_{\ell}\geq r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, then we have shown the desired result. Otherwise, if
\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, then

\displaystyle\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\right\}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\right\}\right)-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\min\left\{r_{i_{k,\ell}},\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell+1}

\displaystyle=\left(r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right)-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)

where the first, second, and third equalities follow from algebra, and the fourth equality follows from the fact that
\delta_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}
. Our proof of Lemma
I.5
is thus complete.
\halmos

Equipped with Lemma
I.5
, we now show that

J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. Indeed, consider any
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. We observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

where the first equality is the definition of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})
, the second equality follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
(which implies that
\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
), the inequality follows from Lemma
I.5
and the fact that
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
(which implies that
x_{i_{k,\ell}}\geq 0
for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}\}
), and the third equality is the definition of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
. We thus conclude that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
.

I.6
Proof that there exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})

In the present Appendix
I.6
, we show that there exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
. Indeed, it follows from the assumption that
{\bm{\delta}}
does not satisfy Property
7
and from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
(see Appendix
I.4
) that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\neq{\bm{\delta}}
. For that to be the case, it follows from Lemma
I.3
that there must exist
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
such that
\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}>\delta_{\ell}
.

Let
\ell^{*}
denote the minimum
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
such that
\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}>\delta_{\ell}
.
It follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{*}
that
\delta^{\prime}_{1}=\delta_{1}
and
\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
, which imply that
\ell^{*}\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
. Let
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
be the vector that is defined for each
\ell\in[N+1]
as

\displaystyle\bar{x}_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\ell\in\{\ell^{*}-1,L_{k}+1\},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

We observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}\}\setminus\{\ell^{*}-1\}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}-1}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}-1}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}-1}^{\prime}\right)\right)1

\displaystyle=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}-1}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}-1}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}-1}^{\prime}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}-1}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}-1}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell^{*}-1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle<\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}-1}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}-1}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}-1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}\}\setminus\{\ell^{*}-1\}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell^{*}-1}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}-1}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell^{*}}-\delta_{\ell^{*}-1}\right)\right)1

\displaystyle=J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})

The first equality follows from the construction of
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
. The second equality follows from algebra. The third equality follows the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
(which implies that
\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
) and from the definition of
\ell^{*}
and Lemma
I.3
(which imply that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,\ell^{*}-1\}
). The inequality follows from the definition of
\ell^{*}
(which implies that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{*}}>\delta_{\ell^{*}}
). The fourth equality follows from algebra, and the fifth equality follows from the construction of
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
. This completes our proof that there always exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
.

Appendix J
Proof of Proposition
4.10

Let
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
satisfy Properties
7
,
7
, and
7
. Let
L
,
\hat{r}
, and
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
be as defined in Subroutine 4, which we repeat below for convenience.

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\triangleq T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle\hat{r}

\displaystyle\triangleq\max\left\{\delta_{L},\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}\right\}

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\triangleq\min\left\{\delta_{\ell},\hat{r}\right\}\quad\forall\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}

Our proof of Proposition
4.10
will make use of the following intermediary lemma.

Lemma J.1

We have
\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}=\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\}
.

Proof J.2

Proof of Lemma
J.1
.
For each
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\}
, we observe that

\displaystyle\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\min\{\delta_{\ell},\hat{r}\}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\ell},\max\left\{\delta_{L},\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\min\left\{\delta_{\ell},\delta_{L}\right\}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\ell}

The first equality is the definition of
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}
. The second equality is the definition of
\hat{r}
. The inequality follows from algebra. The third equality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
and the fact that
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\}
, which implies that
\delta_{L}\geq\delta_{\ell}
. Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\ell},\hat{r}\right\}\leq\delta_{\ell}

where the equality is the definition of
\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}
and the inequality follows from algebra. Combining the above reasoning, we have shown that
\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}=\delta_{\ell}
, which completes our proof of Lemma
J.1
.
\halmos

We will also make use of the following lemma.

Lemma J.3

If
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
, then
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}={\bm{\delta}}
.

Proof J.4

Proof of Lemma
J.3
.
Suppose that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
. Then it follows from the definition of Property
7
that
\delta_{L}=\cdots=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
. Moreover, it follows from the definition of
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
that
\delta_{\ell}>r_{i_{k,\ell}}
for all
\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
. Therefore, we conclude for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
that

\displaystyle\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\},\\
\min\left\{\delta_{\ell},\hat{r}\right\},&\text{if }\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\},\\
\min\left\{\delta_{L},\hat{r}\right\},&\text{if }\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\},\\
\delta_{L},&\text{if }\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\ell}

The first equality follows from the definition of
\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}
and from Lemma
J.1
. The second equality follows from the fact that
L=T_{k}({\bm{\delta}})
and from the supposition that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
, which implies that
\delta_{L}=\cdots=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
. The third equality follows from the fact that
\hat{r}=\max\{\delta_{L},\ldots\}\geq\delta_{L}
. The fourth equality follows from the fact that
\delta_{L}=\cdots=\delta_{L_{k}+1}
. Our proof of Lemma
J.3
is complete.

\halmos

It follows from Lemma
J.3
that if
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
, then
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}={\bm{\delta}}
, which implies that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
and satisfies Properties
7
,
7
,
7
, and
7
. Therefore, assume from this point onward that
{\bm{\delta}}
does not satisfy Property
7
.

The remainder of the proof of Proposition
4.10
is organized as follows. In Appendix
J.1
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
. In Appendix
J.2
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. In Appendix
J.3
, we consider the case where
\delta_{L}\geq\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, and for that case we prove that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Properties
7
,
7
, and
7
as well as dominates
{\bm{\delta}}
. In Appendix
J.4
, we consider the case where
\delta_{L}<\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, and for that case we prove that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Properties
7
,
7
, and
7
as well as dominates
{\bm{\delta}}
.
The combination of these results completes the proof of Proposition
4.10
.

J.1
Proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}

In the present Appendix
J.1
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
. Indeed, it follows from Lemma
J.1
and from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
that
\delta_{1}^{\prime}=\delta_{1}\geq 0
. Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\min\{\delta_{L_{k}+1},\hat{r}\}\leq\delta_{L_{k}+1}\leq\bar{r}_{k}

where the equality is the definition of
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}
, the first inequality follows from algebra, and the second inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
. Finally, we observe for each
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}\}
that

\displaystyle\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\ell},\hat{r}\right\}\leq\min\left\{\delta_{\ell+1},\hat{r}\right\}=\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}

where the first equality is the definition of
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}
, the inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
(which implies
\delta_{\ell}\leq\delta_{\ell+1})
, and the second equality is the definition of
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}
. We have thus shown that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}\in\Delta_{k}
.

J.2
Proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7

In the present Appendix
J.2
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. Indeed, it follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
that
\delta_{1}\leq r_{i_{k,1}}
. Moreover, it follows from Lemma
J.1
that
\delta_{1}^{\prime}=\delta_{1}
. Therefore, we conclude that
\delta_{1}^{\prime}=\delta_{1}\leq r_{i_{k,1}}
, which shows that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

J.3
Case where
\delta_{L}\geq\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}

In the present Appendix
J.3
, we consider the case in which
\delta_{L}\geq\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, and for this case we prove that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Properties
7
,
7
, and
7
as well as dominates
{\bm{\delta}}
. Indeed, assume throughout the remainder of Appendix
J.3
that
\delta_{L}\geq\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
. We will make use of the following lemma.

Lemma J.5

T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\geq L
, and for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\},\\
\delta_{L},&\text{if }\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}\end{cases}

Proof J.6

Proof of Lemma
J.5
.
We observe that

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{L}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L}\leq r_{i_{k,L}}

where the first equality follows from Lemma
J.1
and the second inequality follows from the definition of
L
. We have thus shown that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\geq L
. We conclude the proof of Lemma
J.5
by showing that

\delta_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\prime}=\delta_{L}
for all
\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
. Indeed, we observe for each
\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
that

\displaystyle\delta_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\ell^{\prime}},\hat{r}\right\}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\ell^{\prime}},\max\left\{\delta_{L},\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\ell^{\prime}},\delta_{L}\right\}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L}

The first equality is the definition of
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}
. The second equality is the definition of
\hat{r}
. The third equality follows from the assumption that
\delta_{L}\geq\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
. The fourth equality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
and the fact that
\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
, which together imply that
\delta_{\ell^{\prime}}\geq\delta_{L}
. That completes our proof of Lemma
J.5
.

\halmos

Equipped with Lemma
J.5
, the remainder of Appendix
J.3
is organized as follows. In Appendix
J.3.1
, we prove that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. In Appendix
J.3.2
, we prove that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. In Appendix
J.3.3
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. In Appendix
J.3.4
, we show that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. In Appendix
J.3.5
, we show that there always exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
. The combination of Appendices
J.3.4
and
J.3.5
thus implies that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
dominates
{\bm{\delta}}
.

J.3.1
Proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

In the present Appendix
J.3.1
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. Indeed, it follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
and from the definition of
L
that
L\geq 2
. Moreover, Lemma
J.5
shows that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\geq L
. We thus conclude that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\geq 2
, which shows that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

J.3.2
Proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

In the present Appendix
J.3.2
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.
Indeed, it follows from Lemma
J.5
that
\delta^{\prime}_{L}=\cdots=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}
. Moreover, for each
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L-1\}
, we observe that if
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, then

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell+1}=\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}

The first equality follows from Lemma
J.5
and the fact that
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L-1\}
. The second equality follows from the fact that
\delta_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, the fact that
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L-1\}
, and the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
, which together imply that
\delta_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell+1}
. The third equality follows from Lemma
J.5
and the fact that
\ell+1\in\{3,\ldots,L\}
. We have thus shown for all
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
that if
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}},
then
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}=\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}
, which completes our proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

J.3.3
Proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

In the present Appendix
J.3.3
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. Indeed, it follows from Lemma
J.5
that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\geq L
and that
\delta^{\prime}_{L}=\cdots=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}=\delta_{L}
, which imply that
\delta^{\prime}_{T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})}=\cdots=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}=\delta_{L}
. This shows that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

J.3.4
Proof that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
.

In the present Appendix
J.3.4
, we show that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. Indeed, consider any
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. We first observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell=L}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L}+\sum_{\ell=L}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{L}\right\}-\left(\delta_{L}-\delta_{L}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L}+\sum_{\ell=L+1}^{L_{k}}\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{L}\right\}x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad+\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,L}}-\delta_{L}\right\}x_{i_{k,L}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L}+\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,L}}-\delta_{L}\right\}x_{i_{k,L}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L}+\left(r_{i_{k,L}}-\delta_{L}\right)x_{i_{k,L}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

The first equality is the definition of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})
. The second equality follows from algebra. The third equality follows from Lemma
J.5
. The fourth equality follows from algebra. The fifth equality follows from the fact that
\delta_{L}\geq\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, which implies that
\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{L}\right\}=0
for all
\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}\}
. The sixth equality follows from the definition of
L
, which implies that
\delta_{L}\leq r_{i_{k,L}}
. Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=L}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\sum_{\ell=L}^{L_{k}}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}+\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,L}}-\delta_{L}\right\}x_{i_{k,L}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\sum_{\ell=L}^{L_{k}}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}+\left(r_{i_{k,L}}-\delta_{L}\right)x_{i_{k,L}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\sum_{\ell=L}^{L_{k}}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)1+\left(r_{i_{k,L}}-\delta_{L}\right)x_{i_{k,L}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L}+\left(r_{i_{k,L}}-\delta_{L}\right)x_{i_{k,L}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

The first equality is the definition of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
. The second equality follows from algebra. The third equality follows from the definition of
L
, which implies that
\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}=0
for all
\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{K}\}
. The fourth equality follows from the definition of
L
, which implies that
\delta_{L}\leq r_{i_{k,L}}
. The inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
(which implies that
\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\geq 0
for all
\ell\in\{L,\ldots,L_{k}\}
) and the fact that
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
(which implies that
x_{i_{k,\ell}}\leq 1
for all
\ell\in\{L,\ldots,L_{k}\}
). The fifth equality follows from algebra. Combining the above reasoning, we have shown that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta_{L}+\left(r_{i_{k,L}}-\delta_{L}\right)x_{i_{k,L}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

We thus conclude that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
.

J.3.5
Proof that there exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
.

In the present Appendix
J.3.5
, we show that there exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
.
We will make use of the following lemma.

Lemma J.7

\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
.

Proof J.8

Proof of Lemma
J.7
.
It follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}\leq\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
. Therefore, it follows from the assumption that
{\bm{\delta}}\neq{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
and from the fact that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\}
(Lemma
J.5
) that there must exist
\ell^{*}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
such that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}<\delta_{\ell^{*}}
. We thus observe that

\displaystyle\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}<\delta_{\ell^{*}}\leq\delta_{L_{k}+1}

The first equality follows from Lemma
J.5
(which implies that
\delta_{L+1}^{\prime}=\cdots=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}
). The inequality follows from the construction of
\ell^{*}
. The second equality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
(which implies that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}\leq\cdots\leq\delta_{L_{k}+1}
). We have thus shown that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
, which completes the proof of Lemma
J.7
.

\halmos

Equipped with the above lemma, we now show that there exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
. Indeed, let
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
be the vector that is defined for each
\ell\in[N+1]
as

\displaystyle\bar{x}_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\ell=L_{k}+1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

We observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle<\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle=J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})

The first equality follows from the construction of
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
. The inequality follows from algebra and from Lemma
J.7
. The second equality follows from the construction of
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
. This completes our proof that there always exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
.

J.4
Case where
\delta_{L}<\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}

In the present Appendix
J.4
, we consider the case in which
\delta_{L}<\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, and for this case we prove that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Properties
7
,
7
, and
7
as well as dominates
{\bm{\delta}}
. Indeed, assume throughout the remainder of Appendix
J.4
that
\delta_{L}<\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
. Moreover, let
\bar{L}
denote the largest integer that satisfies
\delta_{\bar{L}}\leq\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
. We will make use of the following lemmas.

Lemma J.9

L\leq\bar{L}<T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})
.

Proof J.10

Proof of Lemma
J.9
.
If follows from the fact that
\delta_{L}<\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
and from the definition of
\bar{L}
that
\bar{L}
must be greater than or equal to
{L}
.

To show that
\bar{L}<T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})
, let
\hat{L}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
denote any integer that satisfies
r_{i_{k,\hat{L}}}=\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
. It follows from algebra that such
\hat{L}
must exist. We will now show by contradiction that
\bar{L}<\hat{L}
. Indeed, suppose for the sake of developing a contradiction that
\hat{L}\leq\bar{L}
. If that were true, then

\displaystyle r_{i_{k,\hat{L}}}<\delta_{\hat{L}}\leq\delta_{\bar{L}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}=r_{i_{k,\hat{L}}}

The first inequality follows from the definition of
L
and from the fact that
\hat{L}>L
. The second inequality follows from the supposition that
\hat{L}\leq\bar{L}
and the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
. The third inequality follows from the definition of
\bar{L}
. The equality follows from the definition of
\hat{L}
.
Since we cannot have
r_{i_{k,\hat{L}}}<r_{i_{k,\hat{L}}}
, it must be the case that
\bar{L}<\hat{L}
.

To complete our proof that
\bar{L}<T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})
, we now show that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\geq\hat{L}
. Indeed, we observe that

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{\hat{L}}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\hat{L}},\hat{r}\right\}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\hat{L}},\max\left\{\delta_{L},\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\hat{L}},\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\hat{L}},r_{i_{k,\hat{L}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=r_{i_{k,\hat{L}}}

The first equality is the definition of
\delta^{\prime}_{\hat{L}}
. The second equality is the definition of
\hat{r}
. The third equality follows from the assumption that
\delta_{L}<\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
. The fourth equality follows from the definition of
\hat{L}
.
The fifth equality follows from the definition of
L
and from the fact that
\hat{L}>\bar{L}\geq L
, which together imply that
\delta_{\hat{L}}>r_{i_{k,\hat{L}}}
. We have thus shown that
\delta^{\prime}_{\hat{L}}\leq r_{i_{k,\hat{L}}}
, which implies
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\geq\hat{L}
. This concludes our proof that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})>\bar{L}
, which completes our proof of Lemma
J.9
.

\halmos

Lemma J.11

For all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\ell\in\left\{1,\ldots,\bar{L}\right\},\\
\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}},&\text{if }\ell\in\{\bar{L}+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}\end{cases}

Proof J.12

Proof of Lemma
J.11
.
We observe for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}

that

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\ell},\hat{r}\right\}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\ell},\max\left\{\delta_{L},\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{\ell},\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{\ell},&\text{if }\ell\in\left\{1,\ldots,\bar{L}\right\},\\
\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}},&\text{if }\ell\in\{\bar{L}+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}\end{cases}

The first equality is the definition of
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}
. The second equality follows from the definition of
\hat{r}
. The third equality follows from our assumption that
\delta_{L}<\max_{\ell\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
. The fourth equality follows from the definition of
\bar{L}
and the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
, which imply that
\delta_{\ell}\leq\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}
for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{L}\}
and
\delta_{\ell}>\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}
for all
\ell\in\{\bar{L}+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
. This completes our proof of Lemma
J.11
.

\halmos

Equipped with the above intermediary lemmas, the remainder of Appendix
J.4
is organized as follows. In Appendix
J.4.1
, we prove that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. In Appendix
J.4.2
, we prove that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. In Appendix
J.4.3

{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. In Appendix
J.4.4

for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. In Appendix
J.4.5
, we show that there always exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
. The combination of Appendices
J.4.4
and
J.4.5
thus implies that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
dominates
{\bm{\delta}}
.

J.4.1
Proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

In the present Appendix
J.4.1
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. Indeed, it follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
and from the definition of
L
that
L\geq 2
. Moreover, it follows from Lemma
J.9
that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})>L
. We thus conclude that
T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})>L\geq 2
, which shows that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

J.4.2
Proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

In the present Appendix
J.4.2
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.
Indeed, it follows from Lemma
J.11
that
\delta^{\prime}_{\bar{L}+1}=\cdots=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}
. Moreover, for each
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,\bar{L}-1\}
, we observe that if
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, then

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell+1}=\delta^{\prime}_{\ell+1}

The first equality follows from Lemma
J.11
and the fact that
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,\bar{L}-1\}
. The second equality follows from the fact that
\delta_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}<r_{i_{k,\ell}}
and the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
, which together imply that
\delta_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell+1}
. The third equality follows from Lemma
J.11
and the fact that
\ell+1\in\{3,\ldots,\bar{L}\}
.
Finally, we observe that

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{\bar{L}}=\delta_{\bar{L}}\geq r_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

The equality follows from Lemma
J.11
. The inequality follows from the definition of
\bar{L}
(which implies that
\delta_{\bar{L}}<\delta_{\bar{L}+1}
and
\bar{L}\geq 2
) and the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
, which imply that
\delta_{\bar{L}}\geq r_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}
. We have thus shown for each
\ell\in\{2,\ldots,L_{k}\}
that if
\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}>r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, then
\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}=\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}
, which shows that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

J.4.3
Proof that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
.

In the present Appendix
J.4.3
, we show that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property
7
. Indeed, Lemma

T_{k}({\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})>\bar{L}
. Moreover, it follows from Lemma

. Therefore, we conclude that

, which shows that
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
satisfies Property

.

J.4.4
Proof that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
.

In the present Appendix
J.4.4
, we show that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
for all
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. Indeed, consider any
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
. We first show that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}+\left(\delta_{\bar{L}}-\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\bar{L}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

(41)

Indeed, we first observe that

\displaystyle\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell=\bar{L}+1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=\bar{L}+1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}\right\}-\left(\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}-\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}

(42)

where the first equality follows from Lemma
J.11
and the second equality follows from algebra. We next observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{\bar{L}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{\bar{L}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

(43)

where the equality follows from Lemma
J.11
. Finally, we observe that

\displaystyle\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}-\delta_{\bar{L}}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\bar{L}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\bar{L}}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}-\delta_{\bar{L}}\right\}-\left(\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}-\delta_{\bar{L}}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

\displaystyle=\left(0-\left(\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}-\delta_{\bar{L}}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\delta_{\bar{L}}-\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

(44)

The first equality follows from Lemma
J.11
. To see why the second equality holds, we observe that Lemma
J.9
implies that
\bar{L}+1\leq L_{k}+1
, and so it follows from the definition of
\bar{L}
that
\delta_{\bar{L}}<\delta_{\bar{L}+1}
. Therefore, it follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
that
\delta_{\bar{L}}\geq r_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}
, which implies that
0\geq r_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}-\delta_{\bar{L}}
. The third equality follows from algebra. Combining (
42
), (
43
), and (
44
), we have shown that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}+\sum_{\ell=\bar{L}+1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}-\delta_{\bar{L}}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\bar{L}+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\bar{L}}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\bar{L}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}+\left(\delta_{\bar{L}}-\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\bar{L}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

where the first equality is the definition of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})
and the second equality follows from (
42
), (
43
), and (
44
). We have thus shown that (
41
) holds.

We next show that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle\geq\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}+\left(\delta_{\bar{L}}-\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\bar{L}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

(45)

Indeed, we first observe that

\displaystyle\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=\bar{L}+1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=\bar{L}+1}^{L_{k}}\left(0-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\geq\delta_{L_{k}+1}-\sum_{\ell=\bar{L}+1}^{L_{k}}\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)1

\displaystyle=\delta_{\bar{L}+1}

(46)

The first equality follows from Lemma
J.9
(which implies that
\bar{L}+1>L
) and the definition of
L
(which implies that
\delta_{\ell}>r_{i_{k,\ell}}
for all
\ell>L
). The inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
(which implies that
\delta_{\ell+1}\geq\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{\bar{L}+1,\ldots,L_{k}\}
) and the fact that
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
(which implies that
x_{i_{k,\ell}}\leq 1
for all
\ell\in\{\bar{L}+1,\ldots,L_{k}\}
). The second equality follows from algebra. Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}-\delta_{\bar{L}}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\bar{L}+1}-\delta_{\bar{L}}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

\displaystyle=\left(0-\left(\delta_{\bar{L}+1}-\delta_{\bar{L}}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\delta_{\bar{L}}-\delta_{\bar{L}+1}\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

(47)

To see why the first equality holds, we observe that Lemma
J.9
implies that
\bar{L}+1\leq L_{k}+1
, and so it follows from the definition of
\bar{L}
that
\delta_{\bar{L}}<\delta_{\bar{L}+1}
. Therefore, it follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}
satisfies Property
7
that
\delta_{\bar{L}}\geq r_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}
, which implies that
0\geq r_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}-\delta_{\bar{L}}
.
The second equality follows from algebra. Combining (
46
) and (
47
), we have shown that

\displaystyle J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

\displaystyle=\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=\bar{L}+1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}-\delta_{\bar{L}}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\bar{L}+1}-\delta_{\bar{L}}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\bar{L}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\bar{L}+1}+\left(\delta_{\bar{L}}-\delta_{\bar{L}+1}\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\bar{L}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle\geq\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}+\left(\delta_{\bar{L}}-\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}\right)x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\bar{L}-1}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)x_{i_{k,\ell}}

where the first equality is the definition of
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})
, the second equality follows from (
46
) and (
47
), and the inequality follows from the definition of
\bar{L}
(which implies that
\delta_{\bar{L}+1}>\max_{\ell^{\prime}\in\{L+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}}r_{i_{k,\ell^{\prime}}}
) and the fact that
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
(which implies that
x_{i_{k,\bar{L}}}\leq 1
). We have thus shown that (
45
) holds.

Combining (
41
) and (
45
), we conclude that
J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})\leq J_{k}({\bm{x}},{\bm{\delta}})

J.4.5
Proof that there exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
.

In the present Appendix
J.4.5
, we show that there exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
.

We will make use of the following lemma.

Lemma J.13

\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
.

Proof J.14

Proof of Lemma
J.13
.
It follows from the construction of
{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}\leq\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
. Therefore, it follows from the assumption that
{\bm{\delta}}\neq{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime}
and from the fact that
\delta^{\prime}_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell}
for all
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,\bar{L}\}
(Lemma
J.11
) that there must exist
\ell^{*}\in\{\bar{L}+1,\ldots,L_{k}+1\}
such that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}<\delta_{\ell^{*}}
. We thus observe that

\displaystyle\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}=\delta_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}<\delta_{\ell^{*}}\leq\delta_{L_{k}+1}

The equality follows from Lemma
J.11
(which implies that
\delta_{\bar{L}+1}^{\prime}=\cdots=\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}
). The first inequality follows from the construction of
\ell^{*}
. The second inequality follows from the fact that
{\bm{\delta}}\in\Delta_{k}
(which implies that
\delta_{\ell^{*}}\leq\cdots\leq\delta_{L_{k}+1}
). We have thus shown that
\delta_{L_{k}+1}^{\prime}<\delta_{L_{k}+1}
, which completes the proof of Lemma
J.13
.

\halmos

Equipped with the above lemma, we now show that there exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
. Indeed, let
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
be the vector that is defined for each
\ell\in[N+1]
as

\displaystyle\bar{x}_{i_{k,\ell}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\ell=L_{k}+1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

We observe that

\displaystyle J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\delta^{\prime}_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}^{\prime}-\delta_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle<\delta_{L_{k}+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{k}}\left(\max\left\{0,r_{i_{k,\ell}}-\delta_{\ell}\right\}-\left(\delta_{\ell+1}-\delta_{\ell}\right)\right)0

\displaystyle=J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})

The first equality follows from the construction of
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
. The inequality follows from algebra and from Lemma
J.13
. The second equality follows from the construction of
\bar{{\bm{x}}}
. This completes our proof that there always exists
\bar{{\bm{x}}}\in\mathcal{X}^{c}
that satisfies
J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}}^{\prime})<J_{k}(\bar{{\bm{x}}},{\bm{\delta}})
.

Appendix K
Implementation Details and Additional Experimental Results

In this appendix, we complement the numerical experiments from Section
5
by discussing implementation details and presenting additional experiments. This appendix is organized as follows.

•

In Appendix
K.1
, we discuss in detail our implementations of the various solution methods from Section
5
.

•

In Appendix
K.2
, we present additional numerical results regarding the synthetic data generation process that is used in Section
5.1
.

•

In Appendix
K.3
, we conduct additional experiments to those in Section
5.1.2
to investigate the practical efficiency of the exclusion set formulation in assortment optimization problems with cardinality constraints.

•

In Appendix
K.4
, we conduct additional experiments to show the out-of-sample performance of the sample average approximation in the context of the real-world dataset from Section
5.2
.

K.1
Implementation details

In Section
5
, our goal is to perform numerical experiments that reflect an honest comparison between the various solution methods proposed in this paper and from the prior literature. In this appendix, we discuss in detail our implementations of the various solution methods from Section
5
, with the aim of transparency as well as reproducibility.

K.1.1
Parameter tuning and optional heuristics.

In our experiments in Section
5
, our goal is to conduct a direct comparison between various solution methods. To this end, we conducted all of our experiments without any optional heuristics or parameter tuning, both for our solution methods as well as the solution methods from the literature. This includes, for example, not tuning the Gurobi mixed-integer programming parameters, not changing the default frequency of callbacks, and not applying any optional heuristics such as the divide-and-conquer warm start from
Bertsimas and Mišić 2019
. The only parameter that we specify is the required numerical tolerance in the original two-phase Benders decomposition method from Section
2.4.2
and our accelerated Benders decomposition method from Section
4
for when to add violated cuts. For both of these decomposition methods, we use a tolerance of
10^{-6}
for detecting a violated cut, as well as a tolerance of
10^{-6}
for terminating the decomposition method.

While optional heuristics and parameter tuning may lead to speedups in the various solution methods, our rationale for not including them are three-fold. First, because our goal in Section
5
is to provide an honest comparison of various solution methods, we do not want the reported computation times to reflect a real or perceived disparity in efforts for choosing optional heuristics and tuning parameters more carefully for some solution methods than others. Second, the spirit of sample average approximation is that it can provide a general approach to solving assortment optimization problems; as such, we believe it would not be ideal for a practitioner to need to select optional heuristics or tune solver parameters separately for different problem instances to obtain good performance. Third, there is a large number of combinations of solver parameters that can be tuned and optional heuristics that could be considered, both for our solutions methods and existing solution methods, making it impractical to conduct a comprehensive comparison of all combinations of optional heuristics and parameter tuning.

K.1.2
Implementation of original two-phase Benders decomposition method.

In Sections
5.1.3
and
5.2
, we report the computation times of the original two-phase Benders decomposition method from
Bertsimas and Mišić 2019
, which is reviewed in Section
2.4.2
and Appendix
A.2
.
Our implementation of their two-phase Benders decomposition method is based on the companion code for
Bertsimas and Mišić 2019
that is made publicly available by the authors.
17
17

17

https://github.com/vvmisic/optimalPLD
, accessed on September 5, 2025.

Upon studying their code, we identified a small typo in their implementation of their two-phase Benders decomposition method. This typo did not affect the correctness of their code; however, this typo made their implementation of their algorithm considerably
slower
than the algorithm described in the main text of
Bertsimas and Mišić 2019
. As we show in Tables
and
below, making a small fix to this typo in their code results in significant speedups in the two-phase Benders decomposition method for the empirical study conducted by
Bertsimas and Mišić 2019
. To provide an honest comparison, all numerical results in Section
5
report the computation time of their implementation of the original two-phase Benders decomposition method with our fix to the typo.

In greater detail, the typo in their implementation concerned their algorithm for generating cuts in Phase 2. Their algorithm for computing optimal cuts for Phase 2 is described in
Bertsimas and Mišić 2019
. In particular, their equation (11a) requires calculating a vector
\bar{{\bm{\beta}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N+1}
where
\bar{\beta}_{\ell}^{k}\triangleq\sum_{j:\sigma_{k}(j)<\ell}\beta^{k}_{j}
for all
\ell\in[N+1]
. This vector can be calculated in
\mathcal{O}(N)
time using the recursion
\bar{\beta}_{\ell}^{k}=\bar{\beta}^{k}_{\ell-1}+\beta^{k}_{\sigma_{k}^{-1}(\ell-1)}
for each
\ell
. However, their code for their implementation of this algorithm calculates
\bar{\beta}_{\ell}^{k}
separately for each
\ell
, which requires
\mathcal{O}(N)
time per
\ell
. This increased the runtime of their Phase 2 algorithm for generating cuts from
\mathcal{O}(KN)
to
\mathcal{O}(KN^{2})
. For this reason, the computation times of their Benders decomposition method reported in
Bertsimas and Mišić 2019
made their method appear slower than it should be.

In all of our numerical experiments in Section
5
, we report computation times for our fixed version of their code that computes cuts in Phase 2 in
\mathcal{O}(KN)
time. To demonstrate the significance of this fix, Tables
and
repeat the numerical results shown in Tables
5.2
and
5.2
from Section
5.2
, appended with additional numerical results that show the computation time of their original implementation of the two-phase Benders decomposition made publicly available by
Bertsimas and Mišić 2019
. These tables show that our small fix to the typo in their code results in 2x-20x speedup in their Phase 2 for the empirical study conducted in
Bertsimas and Mišić 2019
.

K.1.3
Initial cuts in Benders decomposition.

To avoid unboundedness of the outer problem (
5
), both our accelerated Benders decomposition method from Section
4
and the original two-phase Benders decomposition method from
Bertsimas and Mišić 2019
described in Section
2.4.2
initialize the outer problem (
5
) in Phase 1 with a single constraint for each ranking. Consistent with the implementation given by
Bertsimas and Mišić 2019
, the original two-phase Benders decomposition method is initialized with the constraint
q_{k}\leq\max_{i\in[N+1]}r_{i}
for each ranking
k
. Our accelerated Benders decomposition method is initialized with the constraint
q_{k}\leq\max_{\ell\in[L_{k}+1]}r_{i_{k,\ell}}
, which corresponds to the cut that would be obtained by (
13
) from Section
4.1
with
\delta_{1}=\cdots=\delta_{L_{k}+1}=\bar{r}_{k}
.

K.2
Discussion of the data generation process from Section
5.1.1

The data generation process described in Section
5.1.1
consists of generating a "ground truth" ranking-based choice model with
M
rankings (Step 1) and fitting the attraction parameters of the multinomial logit model to transaction data generated from that ranking-based choice model (Step 2). In this appendix, we perform additional experiments to demonstrate the effect of the parameter
M
on the multinomial logit models with rank cutoffs obtained from this data generation process.

The results from these additional experiments are shown in Figure
K.2
. Similarly as in Section
5.1.2
, Figure
K.2
considers a setting with
N=50
products, a rank cutoff of
L=5
, and
M\in\{5,15,25\}
. For each
M\in\{5,15,25\}
, we perform the data generation process described in Steps 1 and 2 of Section
5.1.1
to construct a multinomial logit model with rank cutoffs, and we then generate
\tilde{K}=50000
samples from the constructed multinomial logit model with rank cutoffs by following Steps 3 and 4 of Section
5.1.1
. In Figure
K.2
, we report for each product the proportion of samples in which that product is preferred to the no-purchase option, averaged across ten replications for each
M\in\{5,15,25\}
.
18
18

18

Because the rank cutoff is
L=5
, we observe that at most
5
products are preferred to the no-purchase option in any given sample. As such, for each
M\in\{5,15,25\}
, the sum of the proportions in Figure
K.2
is at most 5.0.
Consistent with the data generation process described in Section
5.1.1
, the products in Figure
K.2
are sorted within each replication so that the attraction parameters satisfy
\nu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\nu_{N}
.

There are two main takeaways from Figure
K.2
. First, we observe that as the number of rankings
M
in the ground truth model increases, the proportion of samples in which each product is preferred to the no-purchase option becomes more evenly distributed across products. This reflects the fact that larger values of
M
leads to a smaller spread of the attraction parameters in the estimated multinomial logit model. Second, we observe that even for the smallest choice of
M
(
M=5
), each product is preferred to the no-purchase option in at least some of the samples.

Experiments from Appendix
K.2
- Visualization of samples.Proportion of samples in which each product is preferred to no-purchase option.

K.3
Additional Experiments from Section
5.1.2

In this appendix, we present additional numerical experiments to those in Section
5.1.2
to explore the impact of cardinality constraints on the computation time of the exclusion set formulation (
12
) and the original mixed-integer programming formulation (
8
).

Our additional experiments follow a similar setup as Section
5.1.2
. Specifically, we use the same data generation process from Section
5.1.1
with
N=50
,
M=5
,
\tilde{K}=10000
, and
L=5
. We then compare the performance of the exclusion set formulation (
12
) and the original mixed-integer programming formulation (
8
) with budget constraints of the form
\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}\leq 5
,
\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}\leq 10
, …,
\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}\leq 45
,
\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}\leq 50
. Table
K.3
shows the computation time for solving (
12
) and (
8
), and it also shows the gap of each method of the root relaxation, i.e., the ratio of the optimal objective values of the linear programming relaxations to the true optimal objective values.

Results for two solution methods under different cardinality constraints. The two solution methods are our exclusion set formulation (
12
) (XSET) and the original mixed-integer programming formulation (
8
) (MIP). The Computation Time columns report the solution time (in seconds) required to solve each formulation under integrality constraints, along with the ratio of these times. The Root Relaxation Gap columns report the average percentage deviation of the root relaxation from the optimal integral solution for both formulations, as well as the ratio of these gaps. Results are averaged over five replications, and results are rounded to two decimal places.

There are three main takeaways from Table
K.3
. First, we observe that the root relaxation gap for both methods decreases as the budget constraint decreases from 10 to 5. This decrease in the root relaxation gaps is consistent with Proposition
3.3
from Section
3.2
, which shows that the root relaxation gap of (
12
) and (
8
) are both 0% if we have a budget constraint of
\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}\leq 1
. Second, we observe that the computation times for solving (
12
) and (
8
) increase as the budget decreases from
50
to
15
, and the computation times then decrease as the budget decreases from 15 to 5. We attribute this to the fact that the root relaxation gaps for both formulations become larger as we decrease from
50
to
15
, after which the root relaxation gaps for both formulations becomes smaller. Third, we observe that the ratio of computation times between (
12
) and (
8
) is closely correlated with the ratio of their root relaxation gaps. This demonstrates the value of the strength of the exclusion set formulation (
12
) compared to the original mixed-integer programming formulation (
8
) from the perspective of reducing the computation time for obtaining an optimal solution.

K.4
Approximation Gap for Section
5.2

In Section
5.2
, we solve the sample average approximation (
2
) using the dataset from
Toubia et al. 2003
, which consisted of
N=3584
products and
\tilde{K}=330
samples. This set of experiments is notably different than the experiments in Section
5.1
for two reasons. First, the dataset from
Toubia et al. 2003
contains far more products than samples, raising potential concerns about the out-of-sample performance of assortments obtained from solving the sample average approximation. Second, the dataset from
Toubia et al. 2003
was obtained using a conjoint analysis rather than Monte-Carlo simulation. As such, it is not possible to obtain more samples to improve the approximation gap of the sample average approximation, nor is it possible to generate an out-of-sample validation set of samples to estimate the approximation gap of the assortments obtained from solving sample average approximation, as was done in Section
5.1.3
.

To address these potential concerns, Table
K.4
presents the results of a five-fold cross-validation conducted on the dataset of
Toubia et al. 2003
from Section
5.2
. Specifically, after randomizing the dataset, we partitioned it into five equal subsets. For each budget constraint, we performed five iterations, using four subsets for the sample average approximation (
2
) and evaluating the revenue performance of the optimal assortment on the remaining hold-out subset. This process allows us to assess how well the optimal assortments generalize to unseen data across different cardinality constraints. To the best of our knowledge, Table
K.4
offers the first empirical analysis of the approximation gap from using the dataset of
Toubia et al. 2003
.

\TABLE

Experiments from Appendix
K.4
- Estimated Approximation Gap.

Constraints

Approximation Gap

\sum x_{i}=2

94.96%

\sum x_{i}=3

95.55%

\sum x_{i}=4

96.99%

\sum x_{i}=5

96.14%

\sum x_{i}=6

97.26%

\sum x_{i}=7

97.46%

\sum x_{i}=8

95.82%

Relative accuracy (100% - MAPE) of optimal assortments from the sample average approximation (
2
), solved using the accelerated Benders decomposition method from §
4
. MAPE is based on the revenue of each optimal assortment on the training data versus its performance on the out-of-sample data. Results are averaged over five-fold cross-validation (Appendix
K.4
) and reported to two decimal places.

Table
K.4
reveals that the approximation gap of the sample average approximation (
2
) on the dataset from
Toubia et al. 2003
is surprisingly small, and substantially smaller than the one reported in Table
5.1.3
. This indicates that this dataset may possess structural properties, potentially stemming from the data collection methodology employed by
Toubia et al. 2003
, that facilitate strong generalization. The consistently low approximation gaps observed on this real-world dataset of
N=3584
products provide additional evidence of the viability of the sample average approximation approach for solving the assortment optimization problems with realistic numbers of products.
